The sample is the loop, not the agent: score it, read the judge's\nreport, close one gap, score it against the same dataset again, and watch the number move.\n\n| File | What it is |\n|---|---|\n| `to_fix_agent.py` | LangChain 1.x / LangGraph ReAct agent for a fictional SaaS company: vector search over a policy handbook plus two deterministic tools. Runnable on its own. |\n| `run_eval.py` | Publishes an AgentX dataset, runs the agent over every case locally inside a real trace, submits the results, and calls `.analyze()` for the LLM-judge report. |\n\nThe tools are sound. The prompt is not — that is the point.\n\n### Setup\n\n```bash\npip install -r ../requirements.txt langchain-anthropic\n```\n\nThe shared `../.env` needs `AGENTX_API_KEY`, `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` for the agent, and\n`OPENAI_API_KEY` for the handbook embeddings. Set `AGENTX_API_BASE_URL` too if you are\npointing at a local API instead of the hosted platform.\n\n### Run the agent\n\n```bash\npython to_fix_agent.py \"Account ACC-4471 wants to cancel and be refunded. How much do they get back?\"\n```\n\nIt prints each tool call, then the answer and token usage.\n\n### Run the evaluation\n\n```bash\npython run_eval.py\n```\n\n7 questions × 2 runs + 2 server-generated smoke-test paraphrases = 16 agent invocations,\nroughly 3 minutes. The script prints the scored report and the dashboard URL.\n\nTo re-run against the same dataset instead of publishing a new one — which is the only way\ntwo runs are comparable:\n\n```bash\nexport AGENTX_DATASET_ID=<id printed at the end of the last run>\n```\n\n### What the evaluation measures\n\nThe dataset's acceptance criteria are strict on purpose: every policy claim grounded in the\nhandbook and cited by doc id, account figures matching the tools exactly, under six\nsentences. Two cases carry a `judge_guideline` that pins the grading to one thing — the\nrefund case must land on `$4,160.14`, and the seat-drop case must answer \"no\". The\nlifetime-license case is a hallucination guard. Jaccard and ROUGE-L similarity are enabled\n(both free, no embedding calls).\n\n### The planted gaps\n\nThe v1 agent is not meant to pass. A baseline run scored **6.5/10, MEDIUM**, with a\n`min 1.0 / max 8.0` spread across runs. Each gap below is a deliberate weakness with a\none-line fix, so you can close them one at a time and watch the score move.\n\n| # | Gap | Where it shows up | Lever |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| 1 | Prompt never requires searching before answering | Seat-drop case scores 1–2: the agent asks for an account ID and never opens the handbook | Add \"call `search_handbook` before answering any policy question\" to `SYSTEM_PROMPT` |\n| 2 | No citation rule | Doc ids appear on some answers, not others; the rubric expects one per claim | Require `[POL-XXX]` next to every borrowed claim |\n| 3 | No tool-discipline rule for money | Refund answers drift between runs (`min 1.0 / max 8.0` spread) | \"Never do refund arithmetic yourself — call `estimate_refund` and quote it\" |\n| 4 | No length or format cap | Emoji bullets, tables and marketing tone against a six-sentence rubric | Add the length cap and \"lead with the direct answer\" |\n| 5 | `TOP_K = 2` | POL-SLA and POL-SEATS compete for the same two slots, so the right doc is sometimes missing | `export TO_FIX_AGENT_TOP_K=3` |\n| 6 | Threshold reasoning | Reads 98.7% as *above* 99.0% and awards a 10% credit instead of 25% | Prompt it to restate the comparison before choosing a tier |\n\nGaps 1–4 and 6 are all edits to `SYSTEM_PROMPT` in `to_fix_agent.py`; gap 5 is an env var.\n\n### The iteration loop\n\n1. Change one lever.\n2. Bump the version tag: `export TO_FIX_AGENT_PROMPT_VERSION=v2`.\n3. Re-run against the same dataset: `AGENTX_DATASET_ID=<id> python run_eval.py`.\n4. Compare the runs in the dashboard — the version tag and the git commit ride along in\n   the run metadata, so each score points back at the code that produced it.\n\nChanging one lever per run is what makes the comparison readable.\n\"\"\"\nEvaluate the Northwind Cloud support agent on AgentX.\n\nPublishes a dataset of support questions, runs the LangChain agent from\n`to_fix_agent.py` against every case locally, submits the results to AgentX for\nscoring, and finishes with `.analyze()` — the LLM-judge report.\n\nEach case is wrapped in a real trace, so the report links every scored answer to the\nexecution timeline that produced it and the judge sees the agent's actual trajectory\nrather than the final string alone.\n\nInstall:\n    pip install agentx-python langchain langchain-anthropic langchain-openai python-dotenv\n\nRun (needs AGENTX_API_KEY, ANTHROPIC_API_KEY and OPENAI_API_KEY in ../.env):\n    python run_eval.py\n\nSet AGENTX_DATASET_ID to re-run against a dataset that already exists instead of\npublishing a new one each time — that is what makes two runs comparable.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport os\nimport subprocess\n\nfrom dotenv import load_dotenv\n\nfrom agentx import AgentX\nfrom agentx.evaluations.models import EvaluationCase\nfrom agentx.integrations.langchain import AgentXCallbackHandler\n\nimport to_fix_agent\nfrom to_fix_agent import MODEL, PROMPT_VERSION, SYSTEM_PROMPT, TOOLS, TOP_K\n\nload_dotenv()\n\nDATASET_NAME = \"Northwind Cloud — Support Agent\"\n\n\ndef _provenance() -> dict:\n    \"\"\"Which code produced this score.\n\n    Without it a run records only \"prompt v2\" — the dashboard truncates the display name,\n    so two runs of the same agent are visually identical and nothing says where the change\n    came from. Everything here is read from git at run time, so it cannot drift from the\n    code that actually ran.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def git(*args: str) -> str:\n        try:\n            return subprocess.check_output(\n                [\"git\", *args], stderr=subprocess.DEVNULL, text=True\n            ).strip()\n        except (subprocess.CalledProcessError, FileNotFoundError):\n            return \"\"\n\n    return {\n        \"git_branch\": git(\"rev-parse\", \"--abbrev-ref\", \"HEAD\") or \"unknown\",\n        \"git_commit\": git(\"rev-parse\", \"--short\", \"HEAD\") or \"unknown\",\n        \"git_dirty\": bool(git(\"status\", \"--porcelain\")),\n    }\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# The agent, wrapped for AgentX: one EvaluationCase in, one result dict out\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\ndef make_run_case(client: AgentX):\n    \"\"\"One EvaluationCase in, one result dict out - with a real ingested trace behind it.\n\n    The result carries `trace_id`, not an inline `trace` payload, and that distinction\n    matters: the engine only renders an agent's execution path into the judge prompt for\n    results that link a real ingested trace. Pass tool calls inline instead and they are\n    dropped, every answer is judged as if the agent had no tools, and the report comes\n    back accusing a perfectly good retrieval step of fabricating its results.\n\n    `sync=True` is required: it blocks until the trace is ingested, which is the only way\n    `span.trace_id` is populated in time to attach it to this result.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def run_case(case: EvaluationCase) -> dict:\n        # The handler detects the enclosing span and emits the chain's graph nodes, LLM\n        # calls and tool calls as real child span rows under it (its _emit_span_tree path),\n        # which is what the Execution Timeline renders. Recording them by hand with\n        # span.add_tool_call() instead folds them into a flat tool_calls column on the one\n        # row, and the timeline has nothing to draw. The outer sync=True span is still what\n        # populates span.trace_id for the result link.\n        handler = AgentXCallbackHandler(client.tracer, name=\"northwind-support-agent\")\n        with client.tracer.trace(\n            \"northwind-support-agent\",\n            input={\"query\": case.query},\n            framework=\"langchain\",\n            model=MODEL,\n            metadata={\"prompt_version\": PROMPT_VERSION, \"retrieval_top_k\": TOP_K},\n            sync=True,\n        ) as span:\n            reply = to_fix_agent.answer(case.query, callbacks=[handler])\n            span.output = reply.text\n\n        return {\n            \"output\": reply.text,\n            \"trace_id\": span.trace_id,\n            \"input_tokens\": reply.input_tokens,\n            \"output_tokens\": reply.output_tokens,\n            \"metadata\": {\n                \"framework\": \"langchain\",\n                \"model\": MODEL,\n                \"llm_calls\": reply.llm_calls,\n                \"tools_used\": \", \".join(reply.tools_used) or \"none\",\n                \"smoke_test_variant\": case.is_smoke_test_variant,\n            },\n        }\n\n    return run_case\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Dataset\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\ndef publish_dataset(client: AgentX):\n    return (\n        client.evaluations.datasets.builder(\n            name=DATASET_NAME,\n            description=(\n                \"Billing, policy and account questions for the Northwind Cloud support \"\n                \"agent. Checks handbook grounding, citation discipline, exact figures \"\n                \"from the refund tool, and refusal to invent products.\"\n            ),\n            number_of_requests=2,\n            acceptance_criteria=(\n                \"The direct answer comes first and is correct. Every policy, pricing or SLA \"\n                \"claim is grounded in the handbook and cites its doc id (e.g. [POL-REFUND]). \"\n                \"Account-specific facts and money figures match the tools exactly. Under six \"\n                \"sentences.\"\n            ),\n            rejection_criteria=(\n                \"Invented plans, prices, discounts, features or guarantees. Formatting and phrasing \"\n                \"differences are not defects.\"\n            ),\n            jaccard_similarity=True,\n            rouge_score=True,\n        )\n        .add_case(\n            query=\"How do I cancel my subscription, and do I keep access until the end of the month?\",\n            expected_results=(\n                \"Cancel from Account > Subscription > Cancel plan. Cites POL-CANCEL.\"\n            ),\n        )\n        .add_case(\n            query=\"Do you have a free trial, and do I need to put in a credit card?\",\n            expected_results=(\n                \"14-day free trial, no credit card required, all Pro features but capped at 5 \"\n                \"seats, extendable once by 7 days. The $250 in add-on credits is excluded because credit \"\n                \"packs are never refundable. Cites POL-REFUND.\"\n            ),\n            judge_guideline=(\n                \"The refund figure must be $4,160.14 and the response must exclude the $250 \"\n                \"add-on credits. Rounding presentation and wording do not matter; a different \"\n                \"dollar figure is a failure.\"\n            ),\n        )\n        .add_case(\n            query=\"Our Pro workspace only had 98.7% uptime last month. Are we owed anything?\",\n            expected_results=(\n                \"Yes — below 99.0% the credit is 25% (Pro is guaranteed 99.9%). The credit must be \"\n                \"claimed within 30 days of the affected month and is applied to the next invoice. \"\n                \"Cites POL-SLA.\"\n            ),\n        )\n        .add_case(\n            query=\"Can I export everything before I cancel, and in what formats?\",\n            expected_results=(\n                \"Yes — Settings > Data > Export, in CSV or JSON. The options are Starter at $19 \"\n                \"and Pro at $49 per user per month, or custom Enterprise pricing, with 20% off for \"\n                \"paying annually. It answers\nbilling, plan and policy questions by grounding every claim in a small handbook\n(vector search) and by delegating account lookups and refund math to deterministic\ntools instead of doing them in the model's head.\n\nThis is deliberately a v1: the tools are sound but the prompt is loose and retrieval\nis narrow, so an evaluation has something real to find. The point of the sample is the\nloop — score it, read the report, close one gap, score it again. README.md lists the\nsix planted gaps and the one-line fix for each.\n\nTools\n  search_handbook(query)                      — semantic search over the policy handbook\n  get_account(account_id)                     — mock account record lookup\n  estimate_refund(plan, seats, days)          — deterministic prorated refund math\n\nInstall:\n    pip install agentx-python langchain langchain-anthropic langchain-openai python-dotenv\n\nRun it directly:\n    python to_fix_agent.py \"How do I cancel, and do I keep access until month end?\"\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport json\nimport os\nimport sys\nfrom dataclasses import dataclass, field\nfrom functools import lru_cache\nfrom typing import Any\n\nfrom dotenv import load_dotenv\nfrom langchain.agents import create_agent\nfrom langchain_anthropic import ChatAnthropic\nfrom langchain_core.documents import Document\nfrom langchain_core.tools import tool\nfrom langchain_core.vectorstores import InMemoryVectorStore\nfrom langchain_openai import OpenAIEmbeddings\n\nload_dotenv()\n\nMODEL = os.getenv(\"TO_FIX_AGENT_MODEL\", \"claude-sonnet-4-6\")\nEMBEDDING_MODEL = os.getenv(\"TO_FIX_AGENT_EMBEDDING_MODEL\", \"text-embedding-3-small\")\n\n# Bump TO_FIX_AGENT_PROMPT_VERSION whenever you change the prompt, so the dashboard\n# can tell two runs of this agent apart. TOP_K = 2 is one of the planted gaps.\nPROMPT_VERSION = os.getenv(\"TO_FIX_AGENT_PROMPT_VERSION\", \"v1\")\nTOP_K = int(os.getenv(\"TO_FIX_AGENT_TOP_K\", \"2\"))\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Knowledge base — the only source of truth the agent is allowed to cite\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\nHANDBOOK: list[tuple[str, str]] = [\n    (\n        \"POL-REFUND\",\n        \"Refunds. Annual plans are refunded in full within 30 days of purchase. After day 30 \"\n        \"an annual plan is refunded pro rata for the unused days of the term. Monthly plans are \"\n        \"not refunded; they simply stop renewing. Add-on credit packs are never refundable.\",\n    ),\n    (\n        \"POL-CANCEL\",\n        \"Cancellation. Cancel from Account > Subscription > Cancel plan. There is no cancellation \"\n        \"fee. Access continues until the end of the billing period already paid for, and the \"\n        \"workspace becomes read-only for 30 days after that before deletion.\",\n    ),\n    (\n        \"POL-TRIAL\",\n        \"Free trial. Export archives \"\n        \"stay downloadable for 90 days.\",\n    ),\n    (\n        \"POL-SLA\",\n        \"Uptime SLA. Pro is covered by a 99.9% monthly uptime guarantee and Enterprise by 99.95%. \"\n        \"If measured uptime falls below the guarantee, the account receives a 10% service credit; \"\n        \"if it falls below 99.0%, the credit is 25%. Credits must be claimed within 30 days of the \"\n        \"affected month and are applied to the next invoice. Starter has no uptime SLA.\",\n    ),\n    (\n        \"POL-SEATS\",\n        \"Seat changes. Seats can be added at any time and are billed pro rata for the remainder of \"\n        \"the term. The evaluation's\n# acceptance criteria are strict, so the gap between the two is what shows up as\n# findings in the report.\nSYSTEM_PROMPT = \"\"\"You are the Northwind Cloud support agent. The evaluation harness uses it to\n    attach AgentX's AgentXCallbackHandler, which turns each graph node, LLM call and tool\n    call into a real child span - the difference between an Execution Timeline showing the\n    actual trajectory and a flat list folded onto one row.
